The efficiency of the primer 895 sets were calculated using 4-fold cDNA dilutions (1:1, 1:4, 1:16, 1:64, and 1:256) in duplicates, 896 while their specificity was checked by analyzing the dissociation curves at temperatures ranging

897 from 60-95 °C.

898 The relative expression of 18 genes in the samples of 2012 was used to validate the RNAseq data.

899 The results linearized with *VvActin* provided slightly better correlation than those linearized with

900 *VvUbiquitin* (Supplemental Figure S3). In addition, *VvActin* had higher expression levels in the 901 noble-rotted and control berries than *VvUbiquitin*, which makes it more suitable for qRT-PCR

902 experiments. Therefore, *VvActin* was selected as reference gene for the subsequent expression 903 analyses using the samples of 2013 and 2014.

## 904 **Enzymatic assays**

905 The extraction procedures for the enzymatic analyses were performed as in Li et al. (2013) with 906 some modifications. For phenylalanine ammonia-lyase (PAL, EC 4.1.3.5) and chalcone 907 isomerase (CHI, EC 5.5.1.6) the extraction was done in 0.5 g of frozen tissue. Tissues were 908 homogenized at 4 ºC in 1 mL of 100 mM Tris–HCl buffer (pH 8.8) containing 14 mM *β*-909 mercaptoethanol, 5 mM DTT, 1% BSA and 5% PVPP. For *trans*-cinnamate 4-monooxygenase 910 (C4H, EC 1.14.13.11) and dihydroflavonol 4-reductase (DFR, EC 1.1.1.219), the same extraction 911 buffer was used as for PAL but with pH 7.5. For chalcone synthase (CHS, EC 2.3.1.74) and 912 flavonone 3-hydroxylase (F3H, EC 1.14.11.9), 0.5 g of frozen tissues were homogenized at 4 ºC 913 with 5% PVPP in 1 mL of 100 mM sodium phosphate buffer (pH 6.8) containing 14 mM *β*-914 mercaptoethanol, 5 mM DTT, 40mM sodium ascorbate, 3 mM EDTA, and 2% BSA at 0–4 ºC. 915 The homogenate was centrifuged at 12,000 × *g* for 20 min at 4 ºC. The supernatant was 916 precipitated with ammonium sulfate (70% w/v saturation), kept on ice for 1 h, and centrifuged at 917 16,000 × *g* for 10 min at 4 ºC. The resulting precipitate was dissolved in 1 mL of 100mM sodium 918 phosphate buffer (pH 6.8) containing 8 mM DTT, 40 mM sodium ascorbate, and 1% m/v BSA. 919 For flavonol synthase (FLS, EC 1.14.11.23) and anthocyanidin synthase (ANS, EC 1.14.11.19), 920 0.5 g of frozen tissues were homogenized with 5% m/v PVPP and 1mL 100mM Tris–HCl buffer 921 (pH 8.0) containing 14 mM *β*-mercaptoethanol, 5 mM DTT, 5mM EDTA, 15mM MgCl2 and 2% 922 m/v BSA. After centrifugation, the enzyme extract was purified with ammonium sulfate as 923 described above. All the above enzyme extracts were desalted by passage through PD10 columns 924 and then used for enzyme analysis immediately.

925 Enzyme quantifications were performed spectrophotometrically using a plate reader with 926 temperature control (Biotek PowerWave HT, Biotek Instruments, USA). PAL activity was 927 assayed in a mixture (250 *µ*L) contained 100 mM Tris-HCl buffer pH 8.0 and enzyme extract. The reaction was initiated by the addition of 150 *µ*L of 200 mg mL-1 928 L-phenylalanine (final concentration 6 mg mL-1 929 ) and the production of cinnamic acid was measured over 10 min at 930 ΔA290. C4H activity was assayed in a mixture (250 *µ*L) contained 100 mM phosphate buffer (pH 931 7.5), 1 mM DTT, 1 mM NADPH and 100 *µ*L enzyme extract. The reaction was initiated by the 932 addition of 10 mM *trans*-cinnamic acid (final concentration 1 mM) and the variation of the 933 absorbance at 290 nm was recorded during 10 min. F3H activity was assayed in two independent 934 reactions following the conversion of naringenin to dehydrokaempferol and the conversion of 935 eridictyol to dihydroquercetin. The reaction mixture (250 *µ*L) contained 100 mM Tris-HCl buffer 936 (pH 7.5), 0.5 mM DTT, 0.25 mM 2-oxoglutarate, 0.05 mM ferrous sulfate, 5 mM sodium 937 ascorbate and 0.5 mM of the substrate (naringenin or eridictyol). The addition of the substrate 938 initiated the reaction and the variation of the absorbance at 280 nm was followed over 10 min. 939 Results obtained from each substrate were combined and plotted as F3H activity. CHI activity 940 was assayed as follows: 2',4,4',6'-Tetrahydroxychalcone was synthesized from naringenin by 941 treatment with 50% KOH followed by acidification and recrystallization from aqueous ethanol. 942 Enzyme extract (100 *µ*L) was added to 50 mM Tris-HC1 pH 7.4 containing BSA (at a final concentration of 7.5 mg mL-1 943 ) and 50 mM KCN to give a final volume of 250 *µ*L mL. The reaction was initiated by adding 5 *µ*L of 1 mg mL-1 944 tetrahydroxychalcone in 2-ethoxyethanol and 945 changes in absorbance at 381 nm was recorded with the cell holder maintained at 30 °C. To allow 946 for spontaneous isomerization of the chalcone the reference cell contained the assay mixture 947 without enzyme. The rate of disappearance of the chalcone (ΔA381), in the presence of enzyme, 948 was used to estimate CHI activity. DFR activity was measured according to Xie et al. (2004) with 949 some modifications. The reaction mixture contained 25 mM Tris-HCl (pH 7.0), 4 mM NADPH, 950 100 mM taxifolin (dihydroquercetin) and enzyme extract. The reaction was initiated by addition 951 of NADPH at 25 °C, followed by measuring the rate of NADPH oxidation at 340 nm. The enzyme activity was calculated by using the extinction coefficient of NADPH, 6.22 mM-1cm-1 952 . 953 CHS and UFGT activities were assayed as described by Ju et al. (1995) and Ghasemzadeh et al.

954 (2012), respectively. Briefly, the reaction mixture for UFGT activity contained 100 *µ*L of enzyme 955 extract, 1 mM UDPGluc dissolved in 20 *µ*L of a 330 *µ*M solution of quercetin in methanol and 956 additional extraction buffer to produce 250 *µ*L total volume. The reaction started by the addition 957 of the quercetin solution and changes in absorbance at 290 nm and 30 ºC were recorded during 10 958 min. Samples were incubated for 15 min at 30 °C. The CHS assay was performed with 20 µL of 959 enzyme extract mixed with 250 mL of 50 mM Tris-HCI buffer, pH 7.6, containing 10 mM KCN. 960 The enzyme reaction was allowed to proceed for 1 min at 30 °C after adding 10 mg chalcone 961 (prepared as described for CHI activity) to 10 *µ*L ethylene glycol monomethylether. The activity 962 was determined by measuring the absorbance at 370 nm.

963 For FLS and ANS activities, the methods of Wellmann et al. (2002) and Pang et al. (2007), 964 respectively, were used. The assay mixture for FSL activity (total volume 300 *µ*L) contained 100 965 *µ*M dihydroquercetin as a substrate, 83 *µ*M 2-oxoglutarate, 42 *µ*M ammonium iron (II) sulfate, 2.5 mM sodium ascorbate, and 2 mg mL-1 966 bovine catalase. FLS activity was calculated by the 967 disappearance of the dihydroquercentin at 330 nm during 10 min. ANS activity was determined 968 for production of cyanidin from leucocyanidin (previously synthetized from eridictyol and 969 NADPH) in a final volume of 250 *µ*L containing 20 mM KPi (pH 7.0), 200 mM NaCl, 10 mM 970 maltose, 5 mM DTT, 4 mM sodium ascorbate, 1 mM 2-oxoglutamate, 0.4 mM FeSO4, 0.4 mM 971 and enzyme extract. Changes in the absorbance at 330 nm and 30 ºC were recorded during 10 min. 972 All the enzymatic activities were expressed as a function of the protein concentration of the

973 extracts, which were assayed and calculated following the method proposed by Bradford (1976).

## 974 **Quantification of total anthocyanin**

975 Samples for total anthocyanin measurements were prepared using 100μg of ground tissue (skin 976 and pulp) suspended in 350 *µ*L of methanol 1% v/v HCl. The samples were homogenized using 977 3-mm stainless steel beads in a TissueLyser II (QIAGEN Inc., USA) for 30 s with a frequency of 978 30/s. The homogenized samples were centrifuged at 10,000 rpm for 10 min and then the 979 supernatant was recovered. The sample extracts were incubated at room temperature for 10 min 980 and then measured spectrophotometrically at 520 nm using a plate reader (Biotek PowerWave HT, 981 Biotek Instruments, Ltd, USA). To calculate the total anthocyanins (μg/gFW of berry) present in 982 the samples, a standard curve was prepared with known amounts of malvidin-3-*O*-glucoside 983 chloride (Sigma-Aldrich Corp.) diluted in methanol 1% v/v HCl. Malvidin-3-*O*-glucoside is the 984 major anthocyanin in grape berries therefore the results are expressed as malvidin-equivalents for 985 comparative purposes.

- 1051 **Table S8.** Enriched grape biological processes (*P* ≤ 0.01) unique to gene clusters with enhanced differential expression (*Padj* 1052 ≤ 0.05) due to *Botrytis* infection.

**Table S9.** <sup>1</sup> 1053 H-NMR spectral data of key metabolites present in noble-rotted Sémillon berries. The 1054 metabolite clusters to which they belong are included. Blue font indicates significant down-regulation, while red font highlights significant upregulation (*Padj* 1061 ≤ 0.05). Gray font denotes genes with fold changes that were not significant (*Padj* 1062 > 0.05). B, Barbera; N, Negroamaro; P, Primitivo; R, Refosco.

### 1090 **FIGURE LEGENDS**

1091 **Figure 1**. **Stages of noble rot in ripe Sémillon berries**. **A**, Representative grape clusters with 1092 asymptomatic berries (control, C) and berries at stages 1 (S1), 2 (S2) and 3 (S3) of noble rot. 1093 Asterisks depict berries with characteristic phenotype of each noble rot stage. **B**, Accumulation of 1094 *Botrytis* biomass in control and noble-rotted berries from three independent harvests. The harvest 1095 of 2013 includes C and S3 berries only. Letters correspond to significant differences between 1096 stages (*P* ≤ 0.01). FW, fresh weight. **C**, Multiple factor analysis (MFA) of infected and control 1097 berries using four types of quantitative variables (represented by vectors): transcript abundance of 1098 24,373 grape genes and 15,550 *Botrytis* genes, accumulation of 2,084 mass features, and *Botrytis* 1099 biomass measurements. Samples used in this analysis were harvested in 2012. Each point 1100 represents a biological replicate for a given stage of noble rot. Ellipses define confidence areas 1101 (95%) for each stage, while squares represent their correspondent center of gravity. DIM, 1102 dimension.

1103 **Figure 2**. **Clusters of significant up-regulated grape genes in response to noble rot. A**, 1104 Patterns of transcript accumulation in the gene clusters. Squares correspond to the median counts 1105 of the genes in control (C) and each noble rot stage: stage 1 (S1), stage 2 (S2) and stage 3 (S3). Asterisks indicate significant (*P*adj 1106 ≤ 0.05) fold changes when comparing each of the stages to 1107 control. **B**, Enriched functional sub-categories (*P* ≤ 0.01) in the clusters. The size of the circles 1108 reflects the number of genes (log10) for each functional sub-category. Complete dataset is 1109 available in Supplemental Table S6.

1110 **Figure 3**. **Clusters of metabolites with significant enhanced accumulation in noble rotted-**1111 **berries. A**, Bar plots show the abundance of some metabolites belonging to clusters 01, 02 and 1112 03. **B**, Patterns of metabolite accumulation in the clusters that included more than 30 metabolites 1113 with significantly higher abundance at any of the noble rot stages compared to control. Squares 1114 correspond to the median abundance of the metabolites in each noble rot stage and control. C, control; S1, stage 1; S2, stage 2; S3, stage 3. Asterisks indicate significant fold changes (\**P*adj 1115 ≤ 0.05, \*\* *P*adj ≤ 0.01, \*\*\* *P*adj 1116 ≤ 0.001) when comparing each of the stages to control. **C**, Number 1117 of metabolites in compound classes and subclasses. Metabolites with unknown identities were not 1118 included. Complete dataset is available in Supplemental Table S7.

1119 **Figure 4**. **Main molecular pathways commonly activated in response to noble rot and**  1120 **healthy ripening. A**, **B**, and **C**, Euler diagrams represent commonly and uniquely up-regulated (*Padj* 1121 ≤ 0.05) genes between *Botrytis* infections of Sémillon grape berries and the ripening of red1122 skinned grape berries, which are enriched (*P* ≤ 0.01) in functional sub-categories of biological 1123 importance (Supplemental Table S12). Circles are scaled to the log10 of the number of genes 1124 included in each of the comparisons. **D**, Venn diagram displaying common and unique upregulated (*Padj* 1125 ≤ 0.05) genes when comparing responses to noble rot in Sémillon berries and the 1126 normal ripening of red-skinned berries, only genes enriched (*P* ≤ 0.01) in grape biological 1127 processes are included. C, control; S1, stage 1; S2, stage 2; S3, stage 3; PreV, *pre-véraison*; V; 1128 *véraison*.

1129 **Figure 5. Activation of the phenylpropanoid metabolism as a common response between**  1130 **noble rot and healthy ripening.** A representation of the central phenylpropanoid and flavonoid 1131 pathways based on KEGG pathways (www.genome.jp/kegg/pathway.html) is provided. Dashed 1132 lines indicate that some steps have been omitted. The gray box indicates an enzyme that has not 1133 been completely characterized and therefore not found in the grape gene annotations. Significant 1134 fold changes (log2) in expression of noble rot responsive genes (Supplemental Table S6) and 1135 ripening genes from five red-skinned cultivars (median value; Supplemental Table S10) are 1136 depicted next to each of the biosynthetic enzymes that they encode. The colors in the heatmap 1137 represent the intensity of the expression changes. C, control; S1, stage 1; S2, stage 2; S3, stage 3; 1138 PreV, *pre-véraison*; V; *véraison*; PAL, phenylalanine ammonia-lyase; C4H, *trans*-cinnamate 4- 1139 monooxygenase; C3H, *p*-coumarate 3-hydroxylase; COMT; caffeic acid 3-O-methyltransferase; 1140 4CL, 4-coumaroyl-CoA ligase; CHS, chalcone synthase; STS, stilbene synthase; CHI, chalcone 1141 isomerase; IFS, isoflavonoid synthase; F3H, flavonone 3-hydroxylase; DFR, dihydroflavanol 4- 1142 reductase; LAR, leucoanthocyanidin reductase; ANS, anthocyanidin synthase; UFGT, UDP-1143 glucose:flavonoid 3-*O*-glucosyltransferase; FLS, flavonol synthase; F3'5'H, flavonoid 3',5'- 1144 hydroxylase; F3'H, flavonoid 3'-monooxygenase.

Asterisks indicate 1149 significant fold changes (\**P*adj ≤ 0*.*05, \*\**P*adj ≤ 0*.*01, \*\*\* *P*adj ≤ 0*.*001). S1, stage 1; S2, stage 2; S3, 1150 stage 3.

A linear trend is shown. The relative 1163 expression data were linearized using *VvActin* as reference gene. **D**, Log2-fold change in total 1164 anthocyanins when comparing *Botrytis*-infected samples against the median value of anthocyanin 1165 levels in the control samples of a given harvest. Figure S1. Summary of quantitative variables from the multiple factor analysis from Figure 2. Only variables that significantly correlated ( $R \ge 0.70$ ,  $P \le 0.01$ ) with the noble-rotted samples (positive dimensions 1 and 2) are visualized. A, Enriched GO terms ( $P \le 0.05$ ) in the set of Botrytis genes that correlated with the positive sides of the first and second dimensions. To highlight closely related enriched GO terms, terms were organized into undirected sub-graphs using the REViGO web server (Supek et al., 2011). Darker colored circles represent terms with lower *P*-values. **B**, Enriched plant biological processes ( $P \le 0.01$ ) among the grape genes that correlated with the infected samples. Circles represent enriched functional sub-categories according to VitisNet (Grimplet et al., 2009). The size of the circles reflects the relative number of correlated genes when compared to all genes in the genome annotated for that functional sub-category. C, Histogram of comclasses represented among the metabolites pound correlated with Botrytis-infected samples. The number of metabolites in each compound class is provided inside each bar. Bars without numbers correspond to one metabolite. DIM, dimension.

38º19'06'' N 122º14'00.03'' W elev. 60 m

**Figure S2.** Map of the vineyard where the noble-rotted and control samples were collected over three years. Yellow lines demark the limits of the vineyard where sampling was performed.

**Figure S3.** Scatterplots showing the correlation between the fold changes  $(\log_2)$  in expression obtained by processing the RNAseq data with DESeq2 and the fold changes  $(\log_2)$  measured by qRT-PCR. Relative expression was calculated using *VvActin* (**A**) and *VvUbiquitin* (**B**) as reference genes. Linear trends, correlation coefficients (*R*) and *P*-values are provided.
